<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove a uniform interior $`C^{1,\alpha_d}`$ estimate for bounded infinity-harmonic functions in every dimension <i>d</i> ≥ 3. For some $`\alpha_d\in(0,1/3]`$ depending only on the dimension, the gradient's supremum norm and <i>α</i><sub><i>d</i></sub>-Hölder seminorm on <i>B</i><sub>1/2</sub> are bounded by a dimension-dependent constant times the oscillation on <i>B</i><sub>1</sub>. The exponent is not explicit, and no endpoint or boundary regularity claim is made. In particular, infinity-harmonic functions are locally <i>C</i><sup>1</sup> on every open subset of ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup>, for <i>d</i> ≥ 3.
> 
> 
> 
> </table>

- **C1** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $d\ge3$ there are constants $\alpha_d\in(0,1/3]$ and $C_d<\infty$ such that every bounded infinity-harmonic function $u\in C(B_1)$, where $B_1\subset\mathbb{R}^d$, belongs to $C^{1,\alpha_d}(B_{1/2})$ and satisfies $$\\|\nabla u\\|_{L^\infty(B_{1/2})} +[\nabla u]_{C^{0,\alpha_d}(B_{1/2})} \le C_d\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_{B_1}u.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the interior gradient norm and its Hölder seminorm by oscillation, with dimension-dependent constants.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-interior-c1)): Let $d\ge3$, let $\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^d$ be a nonempty connected open set, and let $u\in C(\Omega)$ be infinity-harmonic. Then $u\in C^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)$.
  - Explanation: Gives local continuous differentiability on an open domain by rescaling the interior estimate.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-linear-lift)): If $u$ is infinity-harmonic in $\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^d$, then $$U(y,b)=u(y)+b$$ is infinity-harmonic in $\Omega\times\mathbb{R}$.
  - Explanation: Adjoining a linear coordinate preserves infinity-harmonicity.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cone-slopes)): The function $w$ is locally Lipschitz. For a fixed center $z$, both $L_r^+(w;z)$ and $L_r^-(w;z)$ are nondecreasing in $r$. For each fixed allowable radius, they are continuous as functions of the center.
  - Explanation: Cone comparisons supply local Lipschitz regularity and monotone radius-dependent slopes.
- **C5** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-affine-blowups)): Let $w$ be infinity-harmonic in $D$ and let $z\in D$. Every locally uniform limit, along a sequence $r_i\downarrow0$, of $$V_i(Y)=\frac{w(z+r_iY)-w(z)}{r_i}$$ is a linear function on $\mathbb{R}^n$. Such limits exist after passage to subsequences.
  - Explanation: Every subsequential blow-up at a fixed center is linear; this alone does not identify a unique slope.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sum-rule)): Let $g_j$ be continuous on open subsets of Euclidean spaces. Suppose that $\sum_{j=1}^N g_j(z_j)-\Psi(z_1,\ldots,z_N)$ has a local maximum at an interior product point, where $\Psi$ is $C^2$ near that point. Set $A=D^2\Psi$. For every $\eta>0$ there are symmetric matrices $X_j$ such that $$(D_j\Psi,X_j)\in\overline J^{2,+}g_j(z_j),\qquad \operatorname{diag}(X_1,\ldots,X_N)\le A+\eta A^2.$$
  - Explanation: The theorem of sums supplies closed superjets and a joint upper matrix bound at a product maximum.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-three-point)): Let $U_i$ be infinity-harmonic on $B_1\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ with a common Lipschitz bound, and let $\ell_i$ be affine functions such that $$\\|U_i-\ell_i\\|_{L^\infty(B_1)}\longrightarrow0.$$ Then $$\label{eq:three-point} \sup_{\substack{x,y\in B_{1/4}\\x\ne y}} \frac{\|U_i(x)+U_i(y)-2U_i((x+y)/2)\|}{\|x-y\|} \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Affine closeness controls midpoint defects uniformly over all nonzero endpoint separations.
- **C8** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-liouville)): Let $m\ge1$ and $0\le C_0<\infty$. Suppose that $v\in C(\mathbb{R}^m\times\mathbb{R})$ is a viscosity solution of $$\label{eq:limit-pde} F[v]:=D^2v[(\nabla_xv,1)]=0.$$ Suppose, in addition, that for every $z=(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^m\times\mathbb{R}$ and every $R>0$ there are $p\in\mathbb{R}^m$ and $a\in\mathbb{R}$ such that $$\label{eq:limit-fit} \left\|v(x+h,t+\ell)-v(x,t)-p\cdot h -\left(a-\frac{\|p\|^2}{2}\right)\ell\right\|\le C_0R \quad\text{if }\quad \|\ell\|\le R,\quad \|h-p\ell\|\le R.$$ Then $v$ is affine.
  - Explanation: Entire solutions of the limiting equation are affine when uniform sheared-box fits exist at every center and radius.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-family-flatness)): The family modulus in <a href="#eq:flatness-function" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flatness-function">[eq:flatness-function]</a> satisfies $$\label{eq:family-flatness} b(s)\longrightarrow0\qquad(s\downarrow0).$$ Every cylinder fit satisfies $$\label{eq:physical-slope-bounds} \|P\|\le L+\sqrt\lambda,\qquad \|P\cdot e_b-1\|\le\sqrt\lambda.$$
  - Explanation: Cylinder flatness tends uniformly to zero across the normalized family, and fitted slopes satisfy quantitative bounds.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-power-decay)): Assume Theorem <a href="#thm:liouville" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:liouville">8</a>. There are $B\in(0,\infty)$, $\gamma\in(0,1]$, and $s_0\in(0,1/8]$, depending only on $d$, such that $$\label{eq:power-decay} b(s)\le Bs^\gamma\qquad(0<s\le s_0).$$
  - Explanation: The Liouville theorem forces a dimension-dependent power rate for the family flatness modulus.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-bad-scales)): Suppose that <a href="#eq:power-decay" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:power-decay">[eq:power-decay]</a> fails for every positive exponent. There are $\gamma_i\downarrow0$, $s_i\downarrow0$, functions $U_i\in\mathcal U$, centers $z_i\in G$, radii $0<r_i\le s_iD(z_i)$, and parameters $\lambda_i\downarrow0$ such that $\lambda_i$ is not good for $U_i$ at $(z_i,r_i)$ and the following holds. Set $\Lambda=32$. For every fixed $M\ge2$, for all sufficiently large $i$, every center $z$ with $\|z-z_i\|\le Mr_i$ and radius $0<r\le Mr_i$ are admissible, and $\Lambda\lambda_i$ is good for $U_i$ at $(z,r)$.
  - Explanation: Failure of every power rate supplies bad scales with uniformly good enlarged fits nearby.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cylinder-comparison)): Fix $0<c_*\le C_*<\infty$. Suppose two fits for the same function, of the form <a href="#eq:physical-fit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-fit">[eq:physical-fit]</a>, with parameter $\theta^2\in(0,1)$, have slopes $P,P'$ satisfying $c_*\le \|P\|,\|P'\|\le C_*$. Write $n=P/\|P\|$ and $n'=P'/\|P'\|$. If their centers agree and their radii are $H$ and $H'$ with $H\le H'\le2H$, then $$\label{eq:cylinder-comparison} \|P-P'\|+\|n-n'\|\le C\theta,\qquad \bigl\|\|P\|-\|P'\|\bigr\|\le C\theta^2,$$ where $C$ depends only on $c_*,C_*$. The same conclusion holds for equal radii $H$ at centers $z,z'$ if $z'$ lies in the first cylinder with axial and transverse offsets at most $H/4$ and $\theta H/4$, respectively.
  - Explanation: Overlapping cylinder fits control slope directions and their norms at different orders of the fitting parameter.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-nonaffine-limit)): For the sequence supplied by Lemma <a href="#lem:bad-scales" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:bad-scales">11</a> and the normalization <a href="#eq:anisotropic-rescaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:anisotropic-rescaling">[eq:anisotropic-rescaling]</a>, a subsequence of the $v_i$ converges locally uniformly to a nonaffine function $v\in C(\mathbb{R}^d\times\mathbb{R})$ satisfying <a href="#eq:limit-pde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-pde">[eq:limit-pde]</a> and <a href="#eq:limit-fit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-fit">[eq:limit-fit]</a> with one finite constant $C_0$.
  - Explanation: Anisotropic rescaling at bad scales produces a nonaffine entire solution with uniform sheared fits.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-frame-invariance)): Let $v\in\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$, $z_0=(x_0,t_0)$, $R>0$, and $(p_0,a_0)\in\mathbb{R}^m\times\mathbb{R}$. Then $$\label{eq:frame-change} \bar v(y,s)= \frac{v(x_0+R(y+p_0s),t_0+Rs)-v(x_0,t_0)}{R} -p_0\cdot y-\left(a_0+\frac{\|p_0\|^2}{2}\right)s$$ belongs to $\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$. A fit $(p,a)$ for $v$ at the corresponding center and radius $R\rho$ becomes the fit $(p-p_0,a-a_0)$ for $\bar v$ at radius $\rho$.
  - Explanation: Sheared changes of frame preserve the limiting equation and subtract frame parameters from fits.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fit-compactness)): Suppose that $v\in\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ is centered at either $(0,0)$ or $(0,1)$. For $K\ge2$ one has $$\label{eq:centered-growth} \|v(x,t)\|\le C K L(K)^2\qquad ((x,t)\in\mathcal B_K).$$ Every fit $(p,a)$ at a center in $\mathcal B_K$, of any radius $r>0$, satisfies $$\label{eq:fit-parameter-bound} \|p\|+\|a\|\le C\bigl(L(K)+\|\log r\|\bigr).$$ In particular, for a center $(x,t)\in\mathcal B_K$, $$\begin{aligned} \|v(x+h,t)-v(x,t)\| &\le Cr\bigl(L(K)+\|\log r\|\bigr) &&(\|h\|\le r),\label{eq:spatial-fit-modulus}\\ \|v(x,t+\tau)-v(x,t)\| &\le C\|\tau\|\bigl(L(K)+\|\log\|\tau\|\|\bigr)^2 &&(0<\|\tau\|\le\tfrac12).\label{eq:time-fit-modulus} \end{aligned}$$ For either fixed choice of center, every sequence of centered members has a locally uniformly convergent subsequence, and its limit belongs to $\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ and is centered at that same point. Moreover, on every compact time interval these members have uniformly subquadratic growth in $x$: $$\label{eq:uniform-subquadratic-growth} \lim_{M\to\infty}\ \sup_{\substack{v\text{ centered}\\ \|x\|\ge M,\ t\in J}} \frac{\|v(x,t)\|}{\|x\|^2}=0 \qquad(J\Subset\mathbb{R}).$$
  - Explanation: Centered solutions have logarithmic fit bounds, common continuity moduli, compactness, and uniform subquadratic growth.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-quadratic-envelopes)): Suppose that $f$ has the preceding growth property. The extrema in <a href="#eq:envelope-definitions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:envelope-definitions">[eq:envelope-definitions]</a> are attained. The envelopes are continuous jointly in their center and length, including length zero wherever the time arguments remain in $I$. Whenever the expressions are defined, $$\label{eq:envelope-composition} \mathcal{P}_\ell(\mathcal{P}_hf)=\mathcal{P}_{\ell+h}f,\qquad \mathcal{M}_\ell(\mathcal{M}_hf)=\mathcal{M}_{\ell+h}f.$$ If $f$ is a viscosity subsolution of <a href="#eq:limit-pde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-pde">[eq:limit-pde]</a>, then, for every affine path $x(\ell)=x_0+d\ell$ and every fixed $t$, $$\label{eq:path-envelope-convexity} \ell\longmapsto \mathcal{P}_\ell f(x(\ell),t)+\|d\|^2\ell\log\ell \quad\text{is convex}$$ on its allowed range $\ell\ge0$, with $\ell\log\ell=0$ at zero. In particular $\ell\mapsto\mathcal{P}_\ell f(x,t)$ is convex. If $f$ is a viscosity supersolution, then $\ell\mapsto\mathcal{M}_\ell f(x,t)$ is concave. If $f_j\in C(\mathbb{R}^m\times I)$, $f_j\to f$ locally uniformly, and $$\lim_{M\to\infty}\sup_j\sup_{\substack{\|y\|\ge M\\t\in J}} \frac{\|f_j(y,t)\|}{\|y\|^2}=0\qquad(J\Subset I),$$ then $\mathcal{P}_\ell f_j\to\mathcal{P}_\ell f$ and $\mathcal{M}_\ell f_j\to\mathcal{M}_\ell f$ locally uniformly in the allowed centers and positive lengths.
  - Explanation: Quadratic envelopes attain their extrema, compose in length, and obey convexity or concavity dictated by the viscosity sign.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-envelope-slopes)): For a subsolution $f$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:quadratic-envelopes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:quadratic-envelopes">16</a>, $\mathcal{S}_\ell^+f(z)$ is nondecreasing in $\ell$ and $$\label{eq:subsolution-slope-order} \limsup_{\ell\downarrow0}\mathcal{S}_\ell^-f(z) \le\lim_{\ell\downarrow0}\mathcal{S}_\ell^+f(z).$$ The right side may equal $-\infty$. For a supersolution, $\mathcal{S}_\ell^-f(z)$ is nondecreasing and $$\limsup_{\ell\downarrow0}\mathcal{S}_\ell^+f(z) \le\lim_{\ell\downarrow0}\mathcal{S}_\ell^-f(z).$$ Consequently, for a solution both slopes are nondecreasing and $$\label{eq:hamiltonian} \lim_{\ell\downarrow0}\mathcal{S}_\ell^+f(z) =\lim_{\ell\downarrow0}\mathcal{S}_\ell^-f(z) =:H_f(z)\in[-\infty,\infty).$$ The function $H_f$ is upper semicontinuous.
  - Explanation: Envelope slopes are monotone and have a common upper semicontinuous small-length limit for solutions; the limit may be negative infinite.
- **C18** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fitted-envelope-bounds)): For a member $v\in\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ centered at $(0,0)$ or $(0,1)$, $K\ge2$, $z\in\mathcal B_K$, and $0<\ell\le K$, one has $$\label{eq:envelope-slope-bound} \mathcal{S}_\ell^+v(z)\le C L(K)^4,\qquad \mathcal{S}_\ell^-v(z)\le C L(K)^4.$$ Every optimizing displacement for $\mathcal{P}_Kv(z)$ or $\mathcal{M}_Kv(z)$ has length at most $C K L(K)^2$.
  - Explanation: Fits bound envelope slopes and confine optimizing displacements on bounded boxes.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-barycentric-envelope)): Let $f$ have the preceding growth property on $\mathbb{R}^m\times I$. For $s>0$ the supremum in <a href="#eq:barycentric-envelope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:barycentric-envelope">[eq:barycentric-envelope]</a> is attained by a finite configuration, and $$\label{eq:barycentric-duality} \mathcal{C}_s f(x,t)-\frac{\|x\|^2}{2s} =\min_{A\in\mathbb{R}^m}\ \max_{y\in\mathbb{R}^m} \left\{f(y,t)-\frac{\|y\|^2}{2s}-A\cdot(y-x)\right\}.$$ Both extrema on the right are attained. Allowing any probability measure of finite second moment and mean $x$ in <a href="#eq:barycentric-envelope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:barycentric-envelope">[eq:barycentric-envelope]</a> gives the same value. The function $\mathcal{C}_s f(x,t)$ is continuous in $(x,t,s)$ for $s>0$, and $$\label{eq:barycentric-semiconcavity} x\longmapsto\mathcal{C}_s f(x,t)-\frac{\|x\|^2}{2s} \quad\text{is concave}.$$ If $f_j\in C(\mathbb{R}^m\times I)$, $f_j\to f$ locally uniformly, and the sequence has the common growth control $$\lim_{M\to\infty} \sup_j\sup_{\substack{\|y\|\ge M\\t\in J}} \frac{\|f_j(y,t)\|}{\|y\|^2}=0 \qquad(J\Subset I),$$ then $\mathcal{C}_s f_j\to\mathcal{C}_s f$ locally uniformly for $s>0$.
  - Explanation: The barycentric envelope has finite attainment, a dual affine-majorant description, and spatial semiconcavity.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-common-time-subsolution)): Let $v$ be a continuous viscosity subsolution of <a href="#eq:limit-pde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-pde">[eq:limit-pde]</a> on $\mathbb{R}^m\times I$, and fix positive weights $\theta_i$ with $\sum_{i=1}^N\theta_i=1$. Define $$V((y_i)_{i=1}^N,t)=\sum_{i=1}^N\theta_i v(y_i,t).$$ If a $C^2$ function $\Phi$ touches $V$ from above at $(Y_0,t_0)$, where $Y=(y_i)_{i=1}^N$, then $$\label{eq:common-time-inequality} D^2\Phi(Y_0,t_0) \left[\left( \bigl(\theta_i^{-1}\nabla_{y_i}\Phi(Y_0,t_0)\bigr)_{i=1}^N, 1\right)\right]\ge0.$$
  - Explanation: A common-time viscosity test combines weighted subsolutions while preserving the relevant directional inequality.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-barycentric-subsolution)): Let $f\in C(\mathbb{R}^m\times(0,\infty))$ be a viscosity subsolution of <a href="#eq:limit-pde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-pde">[eq:limit-pde]</a> with subquadratic spatial growth on compact time intervals. Then $(x,t)\mapsto\mathcal{C}_t f(x,t)$ is a viscosity subsolution of <a href="#eq:limit-pde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-pde">[eq:limit-pde]</a> on $\mathbb{R}^m\times(0,\infty)$. For every $c\in\mathbb{R}$, the same holds for $(x,t)\mapsto\mathcal{C}_t f(x,t)-ct$.
  - Explanation: The time-dependent barycentric envelope remains a subsolution, including after subtraction of a linear time term.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-envelope-contact)): If $\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ contains a nonaffine member, then there exist $\kappa\in(0,\infty)$ and $v\in\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ centered at $z_*=(0,1)$ for which <a href="#eq:jensen-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:jensen-bound">[eq:jensen-bound]</a> holds with $f=v$ and $I=\mathbb{R}$. For $t>0$ set $$\label{eq:contact-envelope} W(x,t)=\mathcal{C}_t v(x,t)-\frac{\kappa^2t}{2}.$$ This function is continuous, has subquadratic spatial growth on compact positive-time intervals, and is a viscosity subsolution of <a href="#eq:limit-pde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-pde">[eq:limit-pde]</a> on $\mathbb{R}^m\times(0,\infty)$. It satisfies $$\label{eq:contact-sandwich} v(x,t)-\frac{\kappa^2t}{2}\le W(x,t)\le v(x,t) \quad(t>0),\qquad W(z_*)=v(z_*)=0,$$ and $W(\cdot,t)-\|x\|^2/(2t)$ is concave for every $t>0$. At every point of contact with $t>0$, the barycentric gap satisfies $$\label{eq:contact-barycentric-gap} W(x,t)=v(x,t)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \frac{\mathcal{C}_t v(x,t)-v(x,t)}{t}=\frac{\kappa^2}{2}>0.$$ Finally, $$\label{eq:contact-slope} H_v(z_*)\in\mathbb{R},\qquad \lim_{\ell\downarrow0}\mathcal{S}_\ell^+W(z_*)=H_v(z_*).$$
  - Explanation: A nonaffine member yields an extremal contact with a positive barycentric gap and a finite envelope slope.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dp-spatial-regularity)): For every $t,\ell>0$, the optimization defining $f^\ell(x,t)$ has a unique maximizer $y=y(x,t,\ell)$. The function $f^\ell(\cdot,t)$ is differentiable, and $$\label{eq:dp-gradient} \nabla_x f^\ell(x,t)=\frac{y-x}{\ell}.$$ For every $h\in\mathbb{R}^m$ it satisfies the two supporting inequalities $$\label{eq:dp-supports} \begin{split} f^\ell(x,t)+\nabla_x f^\ell(x,t)\cdot h-\frac{\|h\|^2}{2\ell} &\le f^\ell(x+h,t)\\ &\le f^\ell(x,t)+\nabla_x f^\ell(x,t)\cdot h+\frac{\|h\|^2}{2t}. \end{split}$$ In particular, its spatial Hessian is bounded below by $-\ell^{-1}I$ in the semiconvexity sense and above by $t^{-1}I$ in the semiconcavity sense.
  - Explanation: Spatial semiconcavity makes the forward optimizer unique and gives differentiability with quadratic supporting bounds.
- **C24** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-dp-drift)): At the state and steps above define $$\begin{aligned} D&=f(z)+f^{2\epsilon}(z)-2f^\epsilon(z),\\ e&=f^\epsilon(z^-)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\|Q_-\|^2-f(z),\\ \widetilde D&=2g_\epsilon(z)-v(z)-\mathcal{M}_{2\epsilon}v(z),\\ \widetilde e&=v(z)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\|Q_+\|^2-g_\epsilon(z^+). \end{aligned}$$ Here $D$ and $\widetilde D$ are the midpoint gaps for forward convexity and backward concavity. The quantities $e$ and $\widetilde e$ are the gaps from using $z$ as a competitor from the opposite step. All four quantities are nonnegative, and the exact drift identity and the velocity estimate are $$\label{eq:dp-drift} \begin{split} \frac{J_\epsilon(z^+)+J_\epsilon(z^-)}{2}-J_\epsilon(z) &=\frac{D+e+\widetilde D+\widetilde e}{4\epsilon}\\ &\ge\frac{D+e}{4\epsilon}\\ &\ge c\bigl(\|Q_+-Q_0\|^2+\|Q_--Q_0\|^2\bigr), \qquad c=\frac{1}{16(1+\log2)}. \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: The exact drift identity bounds squared discrepancies between optimizer velocities and a common reference velocity.
- **C25** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fitted-frame-covariance)): Let $r>0$, and let $(p_0,a_0)$ be a fit for $v$ at $(x_0,r)$ of radius $r$. At corresponding points define $$\label{eq:contact-frame} \begin{gathered} s=\frac{t}{r},\qquad y=\frac{x-x_0-p_0(t-r)}{r},\\ x=x_0+r\bigl(y+p_0(s-1)\bigr),\qquad t=rs,\\ \bar v(y,s)=\frac{v(x,t)-v(x_0,r)}{r} -p_0\cdot y-\left(a_0+\frac{\|p_0\|^2}{2}\right)(s-1). \end{gathered}$$ Then $\bar v\in\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ is centered at $(0,1)$ and satisfies <a href="#eq:jensen-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:jensen-bound">[eq:jensen-bound]</a> with $f=\bar v$, $I=\mathbb{R}$, and the same $\kappa$. Define $$\bar W(y,s)=\mathcal{C}_s\bar v(y,s)-\frac{\kappa^2s}{2}\qquad(s>0).$$ For every $\sigma>0$, the barycentric and $W$ gaps satisfy $$\label{eq:contact-W-covariance} \begin{split} \mathcal{C}_\sigma\bar v(y,s)-\bar v(y,s) &=\frac{\mathcal{C}_{r\sigma}v(x,t)-v(x,t)}{r},\\ \bar W(y,s)-\bar v(y,s)&=\frac{W(x,t)-v(x,t)}{r} \qquad(s>0). \end{split}$$ For every $\ell>0$, the solution slopes satisfy $$\label{eq:fitted-slope-covariance} \mathcal{S}_\ell^\pm\bar v(y,s)=\mathcal{S}_{r\ell}^\pm v(x,t)-a_0.$$ If the steps in <a href="#eq:dp-step-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dp-step-choice">[eq:dp-step-choice]</a> are chosen at any state with $t>\epsilon$, their images are optimizing steps for the transformed pair, with step size $\epsilon'=\epsilon/r$. Their velocities satisfy $$\label{eq:contact-velocity-covariance} \begin{gathered} Q'_+=\frac{y^+-y}{\epsilon'}=Q_+-p_0,\qquad Q'_- =\frac{y-y^-}{\epsilon'}=Q_--p_0,\\ Q'_0=\nabla_y\mathcal{P}_{\epsilon'}\bar W(y,s)=Q_0-p_0,\qquad Q'_\pm-Q'_0=Q_\pm-Q_0. \end{gathered}$$ All these identities are global at corresponding states, with the indicated positive-time restriction for $W$; the states need not lie in the fitting box. No contact at $(x_0,r)$ is assumed. The centering and fit assertion concerns $\bar v$ alone.
  - Explanation: Fitted frames preserve contact gaps and velocity discrepancies while shifting envelope slopes by the fitted time parameter.
- **C26** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-dp-log-velocity)): Suppose $K\ge2$, $\|x\|,\|t\|\le K$, $0<\epsilon\le1$, and $t>\epsilon$. Then the velocity in <a href="#eq:dp-velocities" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dp-velocities">[eq:dp-velocities]</a> satisfies $$\label{eq:dp-log-velocity} \|Q_0\|=\|\nabla_x\mathcal{P}_\epsilon W(x,t)\| \le C\bigl(L(K)+\|\log t\|\bigr).$$ Here $C$ depends only on the fitting constant, the spatial dimension, and $\kappa$.
  - Explanation: The reference velocity has a logarithmic time bound in every centered fitted frame.
- **C27** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-contact-propagation)): Put $\delta_j=2^{-j}$ for $j\ge1$. There exist a constant $K<\infty$, a point $x_{\rm end}\in\mathbb{R}^m$, and an infinite set $\mathcal J\subset\mathbb N$ with the following property. For every $j\in\mathcal J$ there are a point $x_j\in\mathbb{R}^m$ and a fit $(p_j,a_j)$ for $v$ at $(x_j,\delta_j)$ of radius $\delta_j$, in the sense of <a href="#eq:limit-fit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-fit">[eq:limit-fit]</a>, such that $$\label{eq:contact-propagation-conclusion} \begin{gathered} H_v(x_{\rm end},0)>-\infty,\qquad W(x_j,\delta_j)=v(x_j,\delta_j),\\ \|x_{\rm end}-x_j+p_j\delta_j\|\le K\delta_j. \end{gathered}$$
  - Explanation: Stopped walks and one limiting law localize a common finite-slope endpoint in infinitely many fitted contact frames.
- **C28** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-slope-rigidity)): Let $v\in\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$, with the class and envelopes defined in Section <a href="#sec:limiting-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:limiting-tools">4</a>. Suppose that for some $(b,t_0)\in\mathbb{R}^m\times\mathbb{R}$ and $B\in\mathbb{R}$, $$\label{eq:constant-envelope-slopes} \mathcal{S}_\ell^+v(b,t_0)=\mathcal{S}_\ell^-v(b,t_0)=B \qquad\text{for every }\ell>0.$$ Then $v$ is affine.
  - Explanation: Equal constant envelope slopes at one point and every positive length force affinity.

        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sum-rule",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g_j$ be continuous on open subsets of Euclidean spaces. Suppose that $\\sum_{j=1}^N g_j(z_j)-\\Psi(z_1,\\ldots,z_N)$ has a local maximum at an interior product point, where $\\Psi$ is $C^2$ near that point. Set $A=D^2\\Psi$. For every $\\eta>0$ there are symmetric matrices $X_j$ such that $$(D_j\\Psi,X_j)\\in\\overline J^{2,+}g_j(z_j),\\qquad\n  \\operatorname{diag}(X_1,\\ldots,X_N)\\le A+\\eta A^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-three-point",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U_i$ be infinity-harmonic on $B_1\\subset\\mathbb{R}^n$ with a common Lipschitz bound, and let $\\ell_i$ be affine functions such that $$\\|U_i-\\ell_i\\|_{L^\\infty(B_1)}\\longrightarrow0.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:three-point}\n  \\sup_{\\substack{x,y\\in B_{1/4}\\\\x\\ne y}}\n  \\frac{|U_i(x)+U_i(y)-2U_i((x+y)/2)|}{|x-y|}\n  \\longrightarrow0.$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-liouville",
        "exact_quote": "Let $m\\ge1$ and $0\\le C_0<\\infty$.         "exact_quote": "Suppose that <a href=\"#eq:power-decay\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:power-decay\">[eq:power-decay]</a> fails for every positive exponent.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cylinder-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $0<c_*\\le C_*<\\infty$. Suppose two fits for the same function, of the form <a href=\"#eq:physical-fit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:physical-fit\">[eq:physical-fit]</a>, with parameter $\\theta^2\\in(0,1)$, have slopes $P,P'$ satisfying $c_*\\le |P|,|P'|\\le C_*$. Write $n=P/|P|$ and $n'=P'/|P'|$. If their centers agree and their radii are $H$ and $H'$ with $H\\le H'\\le2H$, then $$\\label{eq:cylinder-comparison}\n  |P-P'|+|n-n'|\\le C\\theta,\\qquad\n  \\bigl||P|-|P'|\\bigr|\\le C\\theta^2,$$ where $C$ depends only on $c_*,C_*$. The same conclusion holds for equal radii $H$ at centers $z,z'$ if $z'$ lies in the first cylinder with axial and transverse offsets at most $H/4$ and $\\theta H/4$, respectively.",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cylinder-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<c_*\\le C_*<\\infty$. Write $n=P/|P|$ and $n'=P'/|P'|$. If their centers agree and their radii are $H$ and $H'$ with $H\\le H'\\le2H$, then $$\\label{eq:cylinder-comparison}\n  |P-P'|+|n-n'|\\le C\\theta,\\qquad\n  \\bigl||P|-|P'|\\bigr|\\le C\\theta^2,$$ where $C$ depends only on $c_*,C_*$. The same conclusion holds for equal radii $H$ at centers $z,z'$ if $z'$ lies in the first cylinder with axial and transverse offsets at most $H/4$ and $\\theta H/4$, respectively.",
          "source_id": "PM-377.1"

      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $0<c_*\\le C_*<\\infty$. Suppose two fits for the same function, of the form <a href=\"#eq:physical-fit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:physical-fit\">[eq:physical-fit]</a>, with parameter $\\theta^2\\in(0,1)$, have slopes $P,P'$ satisfying $c_*\\le |P|,|P'|\\le C_*$. Write $n=P/|P|$ and $n'=P'/|P'|$. If their centers agree and their radii are $H$ and $H'$ with $H\\le H'\\le2H$, then $$\\label{eq:cylinder-comparison}\n  |P-P'|+|n-n'|\\le C\\theta,\\qquad\n  \\bigl||P|-|P'|\\bigr|\\le C\\theta^2,$$ where $C$ depends only on $c_*,C_*$. The same conclusion holds for equal radii $H$ at centers $z,z'$ if $z'$ lies in the first cylinder with axial and transverse offsets at most $H/4$ and $\\theta H/4$, respectively.",
      "explanation": "Overlapping cylinder fits control slope directions and their norms at different orders of the fitting parameter.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-nonaffine-limit",
        "exact_quote": "For the sequence supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:bad-scales\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:bad-scales\">11</a> and the normalization <a href=\"#eq:anisotropic-rescaling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:anisotropic-rescaling\">[eq:anisotropic-rescaling]</a>, a subsequence of the $v_i$ converges locally uniformly to a nonaffine function $v\\in C(\\mathbb{R}^d\\times\\mathbb{R})$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:limit-fit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-fit\">[eq:limit-fit]</a> with one finite constant $C_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-nonaffine-limit",
          "exact_quote": "For the sequence supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:bad-scales\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:bad-scales\">11</a> and the normalization <a href=\"#eq:anisotropic-rescaling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:anisotropic-rescaling\">[eq:anisotropic-rescaling]</a>, a subsequence of the $v_i$ converges locally uniformly to a nonaffine function $v\\in C(\\mathbb{R}^d\\times\\mathbb{R})$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:limit-fit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-fit\">[eq:limit-fit]</a> with one finite constant $C_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-377.1"
        }

      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "For the sequence supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:bad-scales\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:bad-scales\">11</a> and the normalization <a href=\"#eq:anisotropic-rescaling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:anisotropic-rescaling\">[eq:anisotropic-rescaling]</a>, a subsequence of the $v_i$ converges locally uniformly to a nonaffine function $v\\in C(\\mathbb{R}^d\\times\\mathbb{R})$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:limit-fit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-fit\">[eq:limit-fit]</a> with one finite constant $C_0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-frame-invariance",
        "exact_quote": "Let $v\\in\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$, $z_0=(x_0,t_0)$, $R>0$, and $(p_0,a_0)\\in\\mathbb{R}^m\\times\\mathbb{R}$. Then $$\\label{eq:frame-change}\n  \\bar v(y,s)=\n  \\frac{v(x_0+R(y+p_0s),t_0+Rs)-v(x_0,t_0)}{R}\n       -p_0\\cdot y-\\left(a_0+\\frac{|p_0|^2}{2}\\right)s$$ belongs to $\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$. A fit $(p,a)$ for $v$ at the corresponding center and radius $R\\rho$ becomes the fit $(p-p_0,a-a_0)$ for $\\bar v$ at radius $\\rho$.",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },

A fit $(p,a)$ for $v$ at the corresponding center and radius $R\\rho$ becomes the fit $(p-p_0,a-a_0)$ for $\\bar v$ at radius $\\rho$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fit-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $v\\in\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ is centered at either $(0,0)$ or $(0,1)$. For $K\\ge2$ one has $$\\label{eq:centered-growth}\n  |v(x,t)|\\le C K L(K)^2\\qquad ((x,t)\\in\\mathcal B_K).$$ Every fit $(p,a)$ at a center in $\\mathcal B_K$, of any radius $r>0$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:fit-parameter-bound}\n  |p|+|a|\\le C\\bigl(L(K)+|\\log r|\\bigr).$$ In particular, for a center $(x,t)\\in\\mathcal B_K$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n  |v(x+h,t)-v(x,t)|\n    &\\le Cr\\bigl(L(K)+|\\log r|\\bigr)\n       &&(|h|\\le r),\\label{eq:spatial-fit-modulus}\\\\\n  |v(x,t+\\tau)-v(x,t)|\n    &\\le C|\\tau|\\bigl(L(K)+|\\log|\\tau||\\bigr)^2\n       &&(0<|\\tau|\\le\\tfrac12).\\label{eq:time-fit-modulus}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For either fixed choice of center, every sequence of centered members has a locally uniformly convergent subsequence, and its limit belongs to $\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ and is centered at that same point. Moreover, on every compact time interval these members have uniformly subquadratic growth in $x$: $$\\label{eq:uniform-subquadratic-growth}\n  \\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\ \n  \\sup_{\\substack{v\\text{ centered}\\\\ |x|\\ge M,\\ t\\in J}}\n       \\frac{|v(x,t)|}{|x|^2}=0\n  \\qquad(J\\Subset\\mathbb{R}).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fit-compactness",

          "source_id": "PM-377.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-quadratic-envelopes",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $f$ has the preceding growth property. The extrema in <a href=\"#eq:envelope-definitions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:envelope-definitions\">[eq:envelope-definitions]</a> are attained. The envelopes are continuous jointly in their center and length, including length zero wherever the time arguments remain in $I$. Whenever the expressions are defined, $$\\label{eq:envelope-composition}\n  \\mathcal{P}_\\ell(\\mathcal{P}_hf)=\\mathcal{P}_{\\ell+h}f,\\qquad\n  \\mathcal{M}_\\ell(\\mathcal{M}_hf)=\\mathcal{M}_{\\ell+h}f.$$ If $f$ is a viscosity subsolution of <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a>, then, for every affine path $x(\\ell)=x_0+d\\ell$ and every fixed $t$, $$\\label{eq:path-envelope-convexity}\n  \\ell\\longmapsto\n  \\mathcal{P}_\\ell f(x(\\ell),t)+|d|^2\\ell\\log\\ell\n  \\quad\\text{is convex}$$ on its allowed range $\\ell\\ge0$, with $\\ell\\log\\ell=0$ at zero. In particular $\\ell\\mapsto\\mathcal{P}_\\ell f(x,t)$ is convex. If $f$ is a viscosity supersolution, then $\\ell\\mapsto\\mathcal{M}_\\ell f(x,t)$ is concave. If $f_j\\in C(\\mathbb{R}^m\\times I)$, $f_j\\to f$ locally uniformly, and $$\\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\sup_j\\sup_{\\substack{|y|\\ge M\\\\t\\in J}}\n      \\frac{|f_j(y,t)|}{|y|^2}=0\\qquad(J\\Subset I),$$ then $\\mathcal{P}_\\ell f_j\\to\\mathcal{P}_\\ell f$ and $\\mathcal{M}_\\ell f_j\\to\\mathcal{M}_\\ell f$ locally uniformly in the allowed centers and positive lengths.",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-quadratic-envelopes",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $f$ has the preceding growth property. In particular $\\ell\\mapsto\\mathcal{P}_\\ell f(x,t)$ is convex. If $f$ is a viscosity supersolution, then $\\ell\\mapsto\\mathcal{M}_\\ell f(x,t)$ is concave. If $f_j\\in C(\\mathbb{R}^m\\times I)$, $f_j\\to f$ locally uniformly, and $$\\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\sup_j\\sup_{\\substack{|y|\\ge M\\\\t\\in J}}\n      \\frac{|f_j(y,t)|}{|y|^2}=0\\qquad(J\\Subset I),$$ then $\\mathcal{P}_\\ell f_j\\to\\mathcal{P}_\\ell f$ and $\\mathcal{M}_\\ell f_j\\to\\mathcal{M}_\\ell f$ locally uniformly in the allowed centers and positive lengths.",
          "source_id": "PM-377.1"

      "exact_statement": "Suppose that $f$ has the preceding growth property. The extrema in <a href=\"#eq:envelope-definitions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:envelope-definitions\">[eq:envelope-definitions]</a> are attained. The envelopes are continuous jointly in their center and length, including length zero wherever the time arguments remain in $I$. Whenever the expressions are defined, $$\\label{eq:envelope-composition}\n  \\mathcal{P}_\\ell(\\mathcal{P}_hf)=\\mathcal{P}_{\\ell+h}f,\\qquad\n  \\mathcal{M}_\\ell(\\mathcal{M}_hf)=\\mathcal{M}_{\\ell+h}f.$$ If $f$ is a viscosity subsolution of <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a>, then, for every affine path $x(\\ell)=x_0+d\\ell$ and every fixed $t$, $$\\label{eq:path-envelope-convexity}\n  \\ell\\longmapsto\n  \\mathcal{P}_\\ell f(x(\\ell),t)+|d|^2\\ell\\log\\ell\n  \\quad\\text{is convex}$$ on its allowed range $\\ell\\ge0$, with $\\ell\\log\\ell=0$ at zero. In particular $\\ell\\mapsto\\mathcal{P}_\\ell f(x,t)$ is convex. If $f$ is a viscosity supersolution, then $\\ell\\mapsto\\mathcal{M}_\\ell f(x,t)$ is concave. If $f_j\\in C(\\mathbb{R}^m\\times I)$, $f_j\\to f$ locally uniformly, and $$\\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\sup_j\\sup_{\\substack{|y|\\ge M\\\\t\\in J}}\n      \\frac{|f_j(y,t)|}{|y|^2}=0\\qquad(J\\Subset I),$$ then $\\mathcal{P}_\\ell f_j\\to\\mathcal{P}_\\ell f$ and $\\mathcal{M}_\\ell f_j\\to\\mathcal{M}_\\ell f$ locally uniformly in the allowed centers and positive lengths.",
      "explanation": "Quadratic envelopes attain their extrema, compose in length, and obey convexity or concavity dictated by the viscosity sign.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-envelope-slopes",
        "exact_quote": "For a subsolution $f$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:quadratic-envelopes\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:quadratic-envelopes\">16</a>, $\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^+f(z)$ is nondecreasing in $\\ell$ and $$\\label{eq:subsolution-slope-order}\n  \\limsup_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^-f(z)\n       \\le\\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^+f(z).$$ The right side may equal $-\\infty$. For a supersolution, $\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^-f(z)$ is nondecreasing and $$\\limsup_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^+f(z)\n       \\le\\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^-f(z).$$ Consequently, for a solution both slopes are nondecreasing and $$\\label{eq:hamiltonian}\n  \\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^+f(z)\n  =\\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^-f(z)\n  =:H_f(z)\\in[-\\infty,\\infty).$$ The function $H_f$ is upper semicontinuous.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fitted-envelope-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "For a member $v\\in\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ centered at $(0,0)$ or $(0,1)$, $K\\ge2$, $z\\in\\mathcal B_K$, and $0<\\ell\\le K$, one has $$\\label{eq:envelope-slope-bound}\n  \\mathcal{S}_\\ell^+v(z)\\le C L(K)^4,\\qquad\n  \\mathcal{S}_\\ell^-v(z)\\le C L(K)^4.$$ Every optimizing displacement for $\\mathcal{P}_Kv(z)$ or $\\mathcal{M}_Kv(z)$ has length at most $C K L(K)^2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-barycentric-envelope",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ have the preceding growth property on $\\mathbb{R}^m\\times I$. For $s>0$ the supremum in <a href=\"#eq:barycentric-envelope\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:barycentric-envelope\">[eq:barycentric-envelope]</a> is attained by a finite configuration, and $$\\label{eq:barycentric-duality}\n  \\mathcal{C}_s f(x,t)-\\frac{|x|^2}{2s}\n   =\\min_{A\\in\\mathbb{R}^m}\\ \\max_{y\\in\\mathbb{R}^m}\n       \\left\\{f(y,t)-\\frac{|y|^2}{2s}-A\\cdot(y-x)\\right\\}.$$ Both extrema on the right are attained. Allowing any probability measure of finite second moment and mean $x$ in <a href=\"#eq:barycentric-envelope\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:barycentric-envelope\">[eq:barycentric-envelope]</a> gives the same value. The function $\\mathcal{C}_s f(x,t)$ is continuous in $(x,t,s)$ for $s>0$, and $$\\label{eq:barycentric-semiconcavity}\n  x\\longmapsto\\mathcal{C}_s f(x,t)-\\frac{|x|^2}{2s}\n       \\quad\\text{is concave}.$$ If $f_j\\in C(\\mathbb{R}^m\\times I)$, $f_j\\to f$ locally uniformly, and the sequence has the common growth control $$\\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\n  \\sup_j\\sup_{\\substack{|y|\\ge M\\\\t\\in J}}\n       \\frac{|f_j(y,t)|}{|y|^2}=0\n       \\qquad(J\\Subset I),$$ then $\\mathcal{C}_s f_j\\to\\mathcal{C}_s f$ locally uniformly for $s>0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-common-time-subsolution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $v$ be a continuous viscosity subsolution of <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a> on $\\mathbb{R}^m\\times I$, and fix positive weights $\\theta_i$ with $\\sum_{i=1}^N\\theta_i=1$. Define $$V((y_i)_{i=1}^N,t)=\\sum_{i=1}^N\\theta_i v(y_i,t).$$ If a $C^2$ function $\\Phi$ touches $V$ from above at $(Y_0,t_0)$, where $Y=(y_i)_{i=1}^N$, then $$\\label{eq:common-time-inequality}\n  D^2\\Phi(Y_0,t_0)\n   \\left[\\left(\n       \\bigl(\\theta_i^{-1}\\nabla_{y_i}\\Phi(Y_0,t_0)\\bigr)_{i=1}^N,\n       1\\right)\\right]\\ge0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-common-time-subsolution",
          "exact_quote": "Let $v$ be a continuous viscosity subsolution of <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a> on $\\mathbb{R}^m\\times I$, and fix positive weights $\\theta_i$ with $\\sum_{i=1}^N\\theta_i=1$. Define $$V((y_i)_{i=1}^N,t)=\\sum_{i=1}^N\\theta_i v(y_i,t).$$ If a $C^2$ function $\\Phi$ touches $V$ from above at $(Y_0,t_0)$, where $Y=(y_i)_{i=1}^N$, then $$\\label{eq:common-time-inequality}\n  D^2\\Phi(Y_0,t_0)\n   \\left[\\left(\n       \\bigl(\\theta_i^{-1}\\nabla_{y_i}\\Phi(Y_0,t_0)\\bigr)_{i=1}^N,\n       1\\right)\\right]\\ge0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-377.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $v$ be a continuous viscosity subsolution of <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a> on $\\mathbb{R}^m\\times I$, and fix positive weights $\\theta_i$ with $\\sum_{i=1}^N\\theta_i=1$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-envelope-contact",
        "exact_quote": "If $\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ contains a nonaffine member, then there exist $\\kappa\\in(0,\\infty)$ and $v\\in\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ centered at $z_*=(0,1)$ for which <a href=\"#eq:jensen-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:jensen-bound\">[eq:jensen-bound]</a> holds with $f=v$ and $I=\\mathbb{R}$. For $t>0$ set $$\\label{eq:contact-envelope}\n  W(x,t)=\\mathcal{C}_t v(x,t)-\\frac{\\kappa^2t}{2}.$$ This function is continuous, has subquadratic spatial growth on compact positive-time intervals, and is a viscosity subsolution of <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a> on $\\mathbb{R}^m\\times(0,\\infty)$. It satisfies $$\\label{eq:contact-sandwich}\n  v(x,t)-\\frac{\\kappa^2t}{2}\\le W(x,t)\\le v(x,t)\n       \\quad(t>0),\\qquad W(z_*)=v(z_*)=0,$$ and $W(\\cdot,t)-|x|^2/(2t)$ is concave for every $t>0$. At every point of contact with $t>0$, the barycentric gap satisfies $$\\label{eq:contact-barycentric-gap}\n  W(x,t)=v(x,t)\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n  \\frac{\\mathcal{C}_t v(x,t)-v(x,t)}{t}=\\frac{\\kappa^2}{2}>0.$$ Finally, $$\\label{eq:contact-slope}\n  H_v(z_*)\\in\\mathbb{R},\\qquad\n  \\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^+W(z_*)=H_v(z_*).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-envelope-contact",
          "exact_quote": "If $\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ contains a nonaffine member, then there exist $\\kappa\\in(0,\\infty)$ and $v\\in\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ centered at $z_*=(0,1)$ for which <a href=\"#eq:jensen-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:jensen-bound\">[eq:jensen-bound]</a> holds with $f=v$ and $I=\\mathbb{R}$. For $t>0$ set $$\\label{eq:contact-envelope}\n  W(x,t)=\\mathcal{C}_t v(x,t)-\\frac{\\kappa^2t}{2}.$$ This function is continuous, has subquadratic spatial growth on compact positive-time intervals, and is a viscosity subsolution of <a href=\"#eq:limit-pde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-pde\">[eq:limit-pde]</a> on $\\mathbb{R}^m\\times(0,\\infty)$. It satisfies $$\\label{eq:contact-sandwich}\n  v(x,t)-\\frac{\\kappa^2t}{2}\\le W(x,t)\\le v(x,t)\n       \\quad(t>0),\\qquad W(z_*)=v(z_*)=0,$$ and $W(\\cdot,t)-|x|^2/(2t)$ is concave for every $t>0$.           "source_id": "PM-377.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If $\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ contains a nonaffine member, then there exist $\\kappa\\in(0,\\infty)$ and $v\\in\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ centered at $z_*=(0,1)$ for which <a href=\"#eq:jensen-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:jensen-bound\">[eq:jensen-bound]</a> holds with $f=v$ and $I=\\mathbb{R}$. At every point of contact with $t>0$, the barycentric gap satisfies $$\\label{eq:contact-barycentric-gap}\n  W(x,t)=v(x,t)\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n  \\frac{\\mathcal{C}_t v(x,t)-v(x,t)}{t}=\\frac{\\kappa^2}{2}>0.$$ Finally, $$\\label{eq:contact-slope}\n  H_v(z_*)\\in\\mathbb{R},\\qquad\n  \\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^+W(z_*)=H_v(z_*).$$",
      "explanation": "A nonaffine member yields an extremal contact with a positive barycentric gap and a finite envelope slope.",

At every point of contact with $t>0$, the barycentric gap satisfies $$\\label{eq:contact-barycentric-gap}\n  W(x,t)=v(x,t)\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n  \\frac{\\mathcal{C}_t v(x,t)-v(x,t)}{t}=\\frac{\\kappa^2}{2}>0.$$ Finally, $$\\label{eq:contact-slope}\n  H_v(z_*)\\in\\mathbb{R},\\qquad\n  \\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\mathcal{S}_\\ell^+W(z_*)=H_v(z_*).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dp-spatial-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "For every $t,\\ell>0$, the optimization defining $f^\\ell(x,t)$ has a unique maximizer $y=y(x,t,\\ell)$. The function $f^\\ell(\\cdot,t)$ is differentiable, and $$\\label{eq:dp-gradient}\n  \\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)=\\frac{y-x}{\\ell}.$$ For every $h\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$ it satisfies the two supporting inequalities $$\\label{eq:dp-supports}\n\\begin{split}\n  f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h-\\frac{|h|^2}{2\\ell}\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x+h,t)\\\\\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h+\\frac{|h|^2}{2t}.\n\\end{split}$$ In particular, its spatial Hessian is bounded below by $-\\ell^{-1}I$ in the semiconvexity sense and above by $t^{-1}I$ in the semiconcavity sense.",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dp-spatial-regularity",
          "exact_quote": "For every $t,\\ell>0$, the optimization defining $f^\\ell(x,t)$ has a unique maximizer $y=y(x,t,\\ell)$. The function $f^\\ell(\\cdot,t)$ is differentiable, and $$\\label{eq:dp-gradient}\n  \\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)=\\frac{y-x}{\\ell}.$$ For every $h\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$ it satisfies the two supporting inequalities $$\\label{eq:dp-supports}\n\\begin{split}\n  f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h-\\frac{|h|^2}{2\\ell}\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x+h,t)\\\\\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h+\\frac{|h|^2}{2t}.\n\\end{split}$$ In particular, its spatial Hessian is bounded below by $-\\ell^{-1}I$ in the semiconvexity sense and above by $t^{-1}I$ in the semiconcavity sense.",
          "source_id": "PM-377.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every $t,\\ell>0$, the optimization defining $f^\\ell(x,t)$ has a unique maximizer $y=y(x,t,\\ell)$. The function $f^\\ell(\\cdot,t)$ is differentiable, and $$\\label{eq:dp-gradient}\n  \\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)=\\frac{y-x}{\\ell}.$$ For every $h\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$ it satisfies the two supporting inequalities $$\\label{eq:dp-supports}\n\\begin{split}\n  f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h-\\frac{|h|^2}{2\\ell}\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x+h,t)\\\\\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h+\\frac{|h|^2}{2t}.\n\\end{split}$$ In particular, its spatial Hessian is bounded below by $-\\ell^{-1}I$ in the semiconvexity sense and above by $t^{-1}I$ in the semiconcavity sense.",
      "explanation": "Spatial semiconcavity makes the forward optimizer unique and gives differentiability with quadratic supporting bounds.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dp-spatial-regularity",

The function $f^\\ell(\\cdot,t)$ is differentiable, and $$\\label{eq:dp-gradient}\n  \\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)=\\frac{y-x}{\\ell}.$$ For every $h\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$ it satisfies the two supporting inequalities $$\\label{eq:dp-supports}\n\\begin{split}\n  f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h-\\frac{|h|^2}{2\\ell}\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x+h,t)\\\\\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h+\\frac{|h|^2}{2t}.\n\\end{split}$$ In particular, its spatial Hessian is bounded below by $-\\ell^{-1}I$ in the semiconvexity sense and above by $t^{-1}I$ in the semiconcavity sense.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dp-spatial-regularity",
          "exact_quote": "For every $t,\\ell>0$, the optimization defining $f^\\ell(x,t)$ has a unique maximizer $y=y(x,t,\\ell)$. The function $f^\\ell(\\cdot,t)$ is differentiable, and $$\\label{eq:dp-gradient}\n  \\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)=\\frac{y-x}{\\ell}.$$ For every $h\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$ it satisfies the two supporting inequalities $$\\label{eq:dp-supports}\n\\begin{split}\n  f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h-\\frac{|h|^2}{2\\ell}\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x+h,t)\\\\\n  &\\le f^\\ell(x,t)+\\nabla_x f^\\ell(x,t)\\cdot h+\\frac{|h|^2}{2t}.\n\\end{split}$$ In particular, its spatial Hessian is bounded below by $-\\ell^{-1}I$ in the semiconvexity sense and above by $t^{-1}I$ in the semiconcavity sense.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dp-drift",
        "exact_quote": "At the state and steps above define $$\\begin{aligned}\n  D&=f(z)+f^{2\\epsilon}(z)-2f^\\epsilon(z),\\\\\n  e&=f^\\epsilon(z^-)+\\frac{\\epsilon}{2}|Q_-|^2-f(z),\\\\\n  \\widetilde D&=2g_\\epsilon(z)-v(z)-\\mathcal{M}_{2\\epsilon}v(z),\\\\\n  \\widetilde e&=v(z)+\\frac{\\epsilon}{2}|Q_+|^2-g_\\epsilon(z^+).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $D$ and $\\widetilde D$ are the midpoint gaps for forward convexity and backward concavity. The quantities $e$ and $\\widetilde e$ are the gaps from using $z$ as a competitor from the opposite step. All four quantities are nonnegative, and the exact drift identity and the velocity estimate are $$\\label{eq:dp-drift}\n\\begin{split}\n  \\frac{J_\\epsilon(z^+)+J_\\epsilon(z^-)}{2}-J_\\epsilon(z)\n  &=\\frac{D+e+\\widetilde D+\\widetilde e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge\\frac{D+e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge c\\bigl(|Q_+-Q_0|^2+|Q_--Q_0|^2\\bigr),\n  \\qquad c=\\frac{1}{16(1+\\log2)}.\n\\end{split}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dp-drift",
          "exact_quote": "At the state and steps above define $$\\begin{aligned}\n  D&=f(z)+f^{2\\epsilon}(z)-2f^\\epsilon(z),\\\\\n  e&=f^\\epsilon(z^-)+\\frac{\\epsilon}{2}|Q_-|^2-f(z),\\\\\n  \\widetilde D&=2g_\\epsilon(z)-v(z)-\\mathcal{M}_{2\\epsilon}v(z),\\\\\n  \\widetilde e&=v(z)+\\frac{\\epsilon}{2}|Q_+|^2-g_\\epsilon(z^+).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $D$ and $\\widetilde D$ are the midpoint gaps for forward convexity and backward concavity. The quantities $e$ and $\\widetilde e$ are the gaps from using $z$ as a competitor from the opposite step. All four quantities are nonnegative, and the exact drift identity and the velocity estimate are $$\\label{eq:dp-drift}\n\\begin{split}\n  \\frac{J_\\epsilon(z^+)+J_\\epsilon(z^-)}{2}-J_\\epsilon(z)\n  &=\\frac{D+e+\\widetilde D+\\widetilde e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge\\frac{D+e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge c\\bigl(|Q_+-Q_0|^2+|Q_--Q_0|^2\\bigr),\n  \\qquad c=\\frac{1}{16(1+\\log2)}.\n\\end{split}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-377.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "At the state and steps above define $$\\begin{aligned}\n  D&=f(z)+f^{2\\epsilon}(z)-2f^\\epsilon(z),\\\\\n  e&=f^\\epsilon(z^-)+\\frac{\\epsilon}{2}|Q_-|^2-f(z),\\\\\n  \\widetilde D&=2g_\\epsilon(z)-v(z)-\\mathcal{M}_{2\\epsilon}v(z),\\\\\n  \\widetilde e&=v(z)+\\frac{\\epsilon}{2}|Q_+|^2-g_\\epsilon(z^+).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $D$ and $\\widetilde D$ are the midpoint gaps for forward convexity and backward concavity. The quantities $e$ and $\\widetilde e$ are the gaps from using $z$ as a competitor from the opposite step. All four quantities are nonnegative, and the exact drift identity and the velocity estimate are $$\\label{eq:dp-drift}\n\\begin{split}\n  \\frac{J_\\epsilon(z^+)+J_\\epsilon(z^-)}{2}-J_\\epsilon(z)\n  &=\\frac{D+e+\\widetilde D+\\widetilde e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge\\frac{D+e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge c\\bigl(|Q_+-Q_0|^2+|Q_--Q_0|^2\\bigr),\n  \\qquad c=\\frac{1}{16(1+\\log2)}.\n\\end{split}$$",
      "explanation": "The exact drift identity bounds squared discrepancies between optimizer velocities and a common reference velocity.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-dp-drift",

The quantities $e$ and $\\widetilde e$ are the gaps from using $z$ as a competitor from the opposite step. All four quantities are nonnegative, and the exact drift identity and the velocity estimate are $$\\label{eq:dp-drift}\n\\begin{split}\n  \\frac{J_\\epsilon(z^+)+J_\\epsilon(z^-)}{2}-J_\\epsilon(z)\n  &=\\frac{D+e+\\widetilde D+\\widetilde e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge\\frac{D+e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge c\\bigl(|Q_+-Q_0|^2+|Q_--Q_0|^2\\bigr),\n  \\qquad c=\\frac{1}{16(1+\\log2)}.\n\\end{split}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fitted-frame-covariance",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r>0$, and let $(p_0,a_0)$ be a fit for $v$ at $(x_0,r)$ of radius $r$. At corresponding points define $$\\label{eq:contact-frame}\n\\begin{gathered}\n  s=\\frac{t}{r},\\qquad y=\\frac{x-x_0-p_0(t-r)}{r},\\\\\n  x=x_0+r\\bigl(y+p_0(s-1)\\bigr),\\qquad t=rs,\\\\\n  \\bar v(y,s)=\\frac{v(x,t)-v(x_0,r)}{r}\n          -p_0\\cdot y-\\left(a_0+\\frac{|p_0|^2}{2}\\right)(s-1).\n\\end{gathered}$$ Then $\\bar v\\in\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ is centered at $(0,1)$ and satisfies <a href=\"#eq:jensen-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:jensen-bound\">[eq:jensen-bound]</a> with $f=\\bar v$, $I=\\mathbb{R}$, and the same $\\kappa$. Define $$\\bar W(y,s)=\\mathcal{C}_s\\bar v(y,s)-\\frac{\\kappa^2s}{2}\\qquad(s>0).$$ For every $\\sigma>0$, the barycentric and $W$ gaps satisfy $$\\label{eq:contact-W-covariance}\n\\begin{split}\n  \\mathcal{C}_\\sigma\\bar v(y,s)-\\bar v(y,s)\n       &=\\frac{\\mathcal{C}_{r\\sigma}v(x,t)-v(x,t)}{r},\\\\\n  \\bar W(y,s)-\\bar v(y,s)&=\\frac{W(x,t)-v(x,t)}{r}\n       \\qquad(s>0).\n\\end{split}$$ For every $\\ell>0$, the solution slopes satisfy $$\\label{eq:fitted-slope-covariance}\n  \\mathcal{S}_\\ell^\\pm\\bar v(y,s)=\\mathcal{S}_{r\\ell}^\\pm v(x,t)-a_0.$$ If the steps in <a href=\"#eq:dp-step-choice\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dp-step-choice\">[eq:dp-step-choice]</a> are chosen at any state with $t>\\epsilon$, their images are optimizing steps for the transformed pair, with step size $\\epsilon'=\\epsilon/r$. Their velocities satisfy $$\\label{eq:contact-velocity-covariance}\n\\begin{gathered}\n  Q'_+=\\frac{y^+-y}{\\epsilon'}=Q_+-p_0,\\qquad\n  Q'_- =\\frac{y-y^-}{\\epsilon'}=Q_--p_0,\\\\\n  Q'_0=\\nabla_y\\mathcal{P}_{\\epsilon'}\\bar W(y,s)=Q_0-p_0,\\qquad\n  Q'_\\pm-Q'_0=Q_\\pm-Q_0.\n\\end{gathered}$$ All these identities are global at corresponding states, with the indicated positive-time restriction for $W$; the states need not lie in the fitting box. No contact at $(x_0,r)$ is assumed. The centering and fit assertion concerns $\\bar v$ alone.",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dp-log-velocity",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $K\\ge2$, $|x|,|t|\\le K$, $0<\\epsilon\\le1$, and $t>\\epsilon$. Then the velocity in <a href=\"#eq:dp-velocities\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dp-velocities\">[eq:dp-velocities]</a> satisfies $$\\label{eq:dp-log-velocity}\n  |Q_0|=|\\nabla_x\\mathcal{P}_\\epsilon W(x,t)|\n       \\le C\\bigl(L(K)+|\\log t|\\bigr).$$ Here $C$ depends only on the fitting constant, the spatial dimension, and $\\kappa$.",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-contact-propagation",
        "exact_quote": "Put $\\delta_j=2^{-j}$ for $j\\ge1$. There exist a constant $K<\\infty$, a point $x_{\\rm end}\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$, and an infinite set $\\mathcal J\\subset\\mathbb N$ with the following property. For every $j\\in\\mathcal J$ there are a point $x_j\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$ and a fit $(p_j,a_j)$ for $v$ at $(x_j,\\delta_j)$ of radius $\\delta_j$, in the sense of <a href=\"#eq:limit-fit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-fit\">[eq:limit-fit]</a>, such that $$\\label{eq:contact-propagation-conclusion}\n\\begin{gathered}\n  H_v(x_{\\rm end},0)>-\\infty,\\qquad\n  W(x_j,\\delta_j)=v(x_j,\\delta_j),\\\\\n  |x_{\\rm end}-x_j+p_j\\delta_j|\\le K\\delta_j.\n\\end{gathered}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-377.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-contact-propagation",
          "exact_quote": "Put $\\delta_j=2^{-j}$ for $j\\ge1$. There exist a constant $K<\\infty$, a point $x_{\\rm end}\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$, and an infinite set $\\mathcal J\\subset\\mathbb N$ with the following property. For every $j\\in\\mathcal J$ there are a point $x_j\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$ and a fit $(p_j,a_j)$ for $v$ at $(x_j,\\delta_j)$ of radius $\\delta_j$, in the sense of <a href=\"#eq:limit-fit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-fit\">[eq:limit-fit]</a>, such that $$\\label{eq:contact-propagation-conclusion}\n\\begin{gathered}\n  H_v(x_{\\rm end},0)>-\\infty,\\qquad\n  W(x_j,\\delta_j)=v(x_j,\\delta_j),\\\\\n  |x_{\\rm end}-x_j+p_j\\delta_j|\\le K\\delta_j.\n\\end{gathered}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-377.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Put $\\delta_j=2^{-j}$ for $j\\ge1$.           "exact_quote": "Put $\\delta_j=2^{-j}$ for $j\\ge1$. There exist a constant $K<\\infty$, a point $x_{\\rm end}\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$, and an infinite set $\\mathcal J\\subset\\mathbb N$ with the following property. For every $j\\in\\mathcal J$ there are a point $x_j\\in\\mathbb{R}^m$ and a fit $(p_j,a_j)$ for $v$ at $(x_j,\\delta_j)$ of radius $\\delta_j$, in the sense of <a href=\"#eq:limit-fit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-fit\">[eq:limit-fit]</a>, such that $$\\label{eq:contact-propagation-conclusion}\n\\begin{gathered}\n  H_v(x_{\\rm end},0)>-\\infty,\\qquad\n  W(x_j,\\delta_j)=v(x_j,\\delta_j),\\\\\n  |x_{\\rm end}-x_j+p_j\\delta_j|\\le K\\delta_j.\n\\end{gathered}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-377.1"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Let $v\\in\\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$, with the class and envelopes defined in Section <a href=\"#sec:limiting-tools\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:limiting-tools\">4</a>. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Euclidean balls** ([source](#pm-h-3)): All balls are Euclidean; $B_r$ denotes the ball centered at the origin unless a center is specified.
- **Infinity-harmonic function** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A continuous function $u$ on an open set $\Omega$ is *infinity-harmonic* if every local upper $C^2$ test $\phi$ at $z\in\Omega$ satisfies $\Delta_{\infty}\phi(z)\ge0$, and every local lower test satisfies $\Delta_{\infty}\phi(z)\le0$. All tests are included, also when $\nabla\phi(z)=0$.
- **Oscillation and Hölder seminorm** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $\mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_Eu=\sup_Eu-\inf_Eu$, and for a vector field $g$ set $$[g]_{C^{0,\alpha}(E)} =\sup_{\substack{x,y\in E\\x\ne y}} \frac{\|g(x)-g(y)\|}{\|x-y\|^\alpha}.$$
- **Cone slopes** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For an infinity-harmonic function $w$ on an open set $D\subseteq\mathbb{R}^n$, define, whenever $\overline B_r(z)\subset D$, $$L_r^\pm(w;z)=\frac1r\max_{\|h\|\le r}\bigl\{\pm(w(z+h)-w(z))\bigr\}.$$
- **Sheared fitting box** ([source](#sec:reduction)): Its domain is the sheared box $$Q_R(z;p)=\{(x+h,t+\ell): \|\ell\|\le R,\ \|h-p\ell\|\le R\}.$$
- **Limiting solution class** ([source](#sec:limiting-tools)): Write $\mathcal{A}_{m,C_0}$ for the class of continuous functions on $\mathbb{R}^m\times\mathbb{R}$ that solve <a href="#eq:limit-pde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-pde">[eq:limit-pde]</a> and have the fits <a href="#eq:limit-fit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-fit">[eq:limit-fit]</a> at every center and radius.
- **Subquadratic spatial growth** ([source](#eq:envelope-definitions)): We say that a continuous function $f$ on $\mathbb{R}^m\times I$ has *subquadratic spatial growth on compact time intervals* if, for every $J\Subset I$, $$\lim_{M\to\infty} \sup_{\substack{\|x\|\ge M\\t\in J}}\frac{\|f(x,t)\|}{\|x\|^2}=0.$$
- **Quadratic envelopes** ([source](#eq:envelope-definitions)): For $\ell>0$, with the indicated time argument in $I$, define $$\label{eq:envelope-definitions} \begin{split} \mathcal{P}_\ell f(x,t) &=\sup_y\left\{f(y,t+\ell)-\frac{\|y-x\|^2}{2\ell}\right\},\\ \mathcal{M}_\ell f(x,t) &=\inf_y\left\{f(y,t-\ell)+\frac{\|y-x\|^2}{2\ell}\right\}. \end{split}$$
- **Barycentric envelope** ([source](#eq:barycentric-envelope)): For a continuous function $f$ on $\mathbb{R}^m\times I$ with subquadratic spatial growth on compact time intervals, define, for $s>0$, $$\label{eq:barycentric-envelope} \mathcal{C}_s f(x,t)= \sup_{\substack{N,\ \theta_i>0,\ \sum_i\theta_i=1\\ \sum_i\theta_i y_i=x}} \left\{\sum_i\theta_i f(y_i,t) -\frac{\sum_i\theta_i\|y_i-x\|^2}{2s}\right\}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Spatial dimension | ≥ | $3$ | Integer dimension. |
| Q2 | Interior gradient Hölder exponent | ≤ | $1/3$ | The positive exponent depends only on dimension. |
| Q3 | Interior estimate constant | < | $\infty$ | Dimension-dependent finite constant; explicit value is absent. |
| Q4 | Target ball radius | = | $1/2$ | Concentric interior ball. |
| Q5 | Gradient Hölder exponent | = | $\frac{\gamma}{2+\gamma}$ | Power decay exponent in the source range. |
| Q6 | Flatness modulus | ≤ | $Bs^\gamma$ | Uniform normalized family; constants depend only on dimension. |
| Q7 | Enlarged fitting parameter multiplier | = | $32$ | Each fixed neighborhood multiplier has its own eventual threshold. |
| Q8 | Inherited fitting constant | = | $256$ | Entire anisotropic limit obtained from bad scales. |
| Q9 | Barycentric envelope gap | ≤ | $\frac{\kappa^2s}{2}$ | Positive penalty scale under the global Jensen bound. |
| Q10 | Drift coefficient | = | $\frac{1}{16(1+\log2)}$ | Forward and backward optimizer steps at positive time exceeding step length. |
| Q11 | Lower time-level hitting probability | ≥ | $\frac34$ | Stopped walks after the outer cutoffs are chosen. |
| Q12 | Fitted frame localization probability | ≥ | $\frac12$ | Uniformly over marked levels of each stopped walk. |

The quantities $e$ and $\\widetilde e$ are the gaps from using $z$ as a competitor from the opposite step. All four quantities are nonnegative, and the exact drift identity and the velocity estimate are $$\\label{eq:dp-drift}\n\\begin{split}\n  \\frac{J_\\epsilon(z^+)+J_\\epsilon(z^-)}{2}-J_\\epsilon(z)\n  &=\\frac{D+e+\\widetilde D+\\widetilde e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge\\frac{D+e}{4\\epsilon}\\\\\n  &\\ge c\\bigl(|Q_+-Q_0|^2+|Q_--Q_0|^2\\bigr),\n  \\qquad c=\\frac{1}{16(1+\\log2)}.\n\\end{split}$$",
            "source_id": "PM-377.1"

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-three-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-three-point): Cone comparisons, affine blow-ups, and viscosity matrix tests. -> Uniform midpoint-defect control.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-family-flatness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-family-flatness): Slow-scale compactness transfers midpoint identities to smaller scales. -> A common qualitative flatness modulus.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-nonaffine-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nonaffine-limit): Select bad scales, compare cylinder fits, and stretch transverse coordinates. -> A nonaffine entire limiting solution with uniform sheared fits.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-envelope-contact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-envelope-contact): Fit compactness, finite barycentric duality, and common-time subsolution tests. -> An extremal solution and subsolution contact with positive barycentric gap.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-contact-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-contact-propagation): Stopped symmetric walks, drift control, and a joint weak limiting law. -> A common finite-slope endpoint localized in infinitely many contact frames.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-slope-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slope-rigidity): Directed quadratic comparisons identify common equality velocities. -> Affinity from constant envelope slopes at all lengths.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-liouville) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slope-rigidity-2): Normalize shrinking contact frames and cancel affine slope shifts. -> The retained positive barycentric gap contradicts affinity.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-power-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nonaffine-limit-2): Contradict the nonaffine bad-scale limit using the Liouville classification. -> Uniform power decay for the flatness modulus.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nonaffine-limit-3): Sum consecutive slope errors and compare uniform Taylor estimates. -> The uniform interior gradient estimate.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-cor-interior-c1) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-interior-c1): Rescale a closed interior ball and apply the uniform estimate. -> Local continuous differentiability.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: Bounded continuous infinity-harmonic functions in dimensions $d\ge3$ satisfy a uniform interior gradient bound and Hölder estimate controlled by oscillation.
- **I2**: Adjoining a linear coordinate preserves infinity-harmonicity.
- **I3**: An entire viscosity solution of the limiting equation with the stated sheared affine fits is affine.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Use the interior estimate as a boundary regularity guarantee. -> The controlled region is strictly interior; boundary behavior is outside this estimate.
- **A2**: Assign the endpoint exponent $1/3$ to every solution covered by the theorem. -> An upper bound on the allowed exponent does not identify its value.
- **A3**: Certify a discrete approximation merely from smooth-looking gradients. -> Finite sample behavior does not establish the viscosity equation for a continuous function.
- **A4**: Infer affinity from the limiting equation alone or from fits on a sampled region. -> The Liouville hypothesis imposes fits at every center and every positive radius in the entire space.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The interior estimate concerns continuous viscosity solutions, and its exponent arises from compactness and is not explicit. The Liouville conclusion requires affine fits at every center and radius on the entire space. These analytical results provide no finite constructive procedure for computing the exponent, the dimension-dependent constant, or a solution; finite tests cannot decide the stated continuum hypotheses.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The uniform gradient estimate requires a bounded continuous infinity-harmonic function in the stated Euclidean dimension regime and controls a strictly interior ball. Blocked use: ['Boundary regularity guarantees', 'Application without continuity, boundedness, or the infinity-harmonic equation', 'Extension to dimensions outside the main theorem']
- **L2** (nonconstructive): The regularity exponent is dimension-dependent and nonexplicit; the theorem does not supply an explicit numerical tolerance or a finite procedure for computing its constants. Blocked use: ['Assigning a computable exponent or norm constant', 'Turning the existence estimate into a solver stopping criterion']
- **L3** (counterexample_scope): The singular infinity-harmonic example excludes exponents above the stated ceiling, while the compactness argument does not establish attainment of the endpoint. Blocked use: ['Stronger gradient Hölder exponents', 'Universal endpoint regularity or endpoint norm estimates']
- **L4** (hypothesis): The viscosity definition includes tests with vanishing gradient; checking only nonzero-gradient tests changes the required hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Discarding critical-point tests', 'Certifying infinity-harmonicity from selected smooth-point residuals']
- **L5** (approximation): Continuum interior regularity alone supplies no discretization error estimate or convergence certificate for sampled approximations. A validated connection to a numerical output is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Certifying a sampled solver from smooth-looking gradients', 'Inferring an approximation error or runtime bound']
- **L6** (hypothesis): Liouville rigidity requires an entire viscosity solution and uniform sheared affine fits at every center and radius. The limiting equation alone does not imply the stated conclusion. Blocked use: ['Applying rigidity on a bounded domain', 'Dropping the affine-fit hypothesis', 'Replacing all centers and radii by finitely sampled fits']
- **L7** (edge_case): The common envelope-slope limit may be negative infinity; finiteness cannot be assumed for a general solution solely from monotonicity. Blocked use: ['Treating every envelope-slope limit as a real-valued observable']
- **L8** (hypothesis): Quadratic-envelope attainment and continuity are stated under subquadratic spatial growth on compact time intervals. These conclusions cannot be transferred to arbitrary growth classes. Blocked use: ['Unconstrained quadratic optimization over arbitrary continuous functions']
- **L9** (nonconstructive): The contact-propagation proof selects optimizers and passes to an infinite limiting law. It does not specify a finite terminating algorithm for finding the terminal point or selected fits. Blocked use: ['Treating the proof walk as a finite contact-finding algorithm', 'Reporting finite simulation as a certificate of the entire theorem']
- **L10** (formal_scope): The result concerns regularity of scalar continuum solutions. A formal bridge to security mechanisms, compiler transformations, or general software correctness is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Kernel isolation guarantees', 'Compiler correctness guarantees', 'Distributed protocol correctness guarantees']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove a uniform interior $`C^{1,\alpha_d}`$ estimate for bounded infinity-harmonic functions in every dimension <i>d</i> ≥ 3. For some $`\alpha_d\in(0,1/3]`$ depending only on the dimension, the gradient's supremum norm and <i>α</i><sub><i>d</i></sub>-Hölder seminorm on <i>B</i><sub>1/2</sub> are bounded by a dimension-dependent constant times the oscillation on <i>B</i><sub>1</sub>. The exponent is not explicit, and no endpoint or boundary regularity claim is made. In particular, infinity-harmonic functions are locally <i>C</i><sup>1</sup> on every open subset of ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup>, for <i>d</i> ≥ 3.



</table>
